01. Operational mandate and baseline

Aperture must make enterprise AI services safe to change, understandable to investigate and predictable to operate. Operational quality is measured by correct user outcomes and controlled exposure, not the number of charts or an all-green process dashboard. This review extends Documentation Baseline C1 without changing runtime application code, prior ADR text or repository state.

Decision

Adopt this material as a versioned operational contract and qualification backlog. Use the implemented probes, protected records and SOP commands now. Enable proposed exporters, dashboards and alarms only after their data, scope and failure semantics are qualified.

SurfaceObserved baselineWhat this review suppliesWhat it does not establish
Persistent referenceAperture R14; schema 2; operations-v1, signals-v1 and notifications-v1; SQLite DELETE journal.Source-grounded diagnostics and runbooks.Production availability, distributed authority or real inference.
Application evidenceFive health probes, durable request/financial/audit records, cases/outbox and ASGI access JSON.Complete evidence inventory; 178 canonical metric contracts.An installed /metrics endpoint, OTLP collector or native tracing.
LiteLLM forkPinned c8ed5cc… baseline; correction PR and adapter candidate remain separate artifacts.85 declared native metric names plus 27 live-document supplementary families.Actual configured exposition, all runtime/plugin metrics, or current PR approval.
Monitoring stackNo Prometheus, Alertmanager or Loki service provisioned by this document.48 alarm templates, nine recording rules, five rule fixtures and 100 query recipes.Live paging, PromQL/LogQL server execution or validated external data.
Operator experienceNine server-backed views and fifteen-screen standalone demo are distinct.12 dashboard specifications and 40 SOPs tied to evidence.Representative-user study results or accessibility certification.

“Complete” here means complete coverage of the enumerated R14 operating surfaces, the canonical 178-family contract, the inspected LiteLLM name declaration and the listed SOP lifecycle. A deployment can add provider, host, SDK, process and plugin metrics. Capture and diff its actual authorized exposition before accepting the inventory as exhaustive for that environment. SRC-01 SRC-03 SRC-06 SRC-10 SRC-13

Research cut: 21 October 2026. R14 source files are preserved and hash-referenced. Live vendor documentation is labeled separately from pinned source. Historical release test counts are not rerun or added to this review’s validation. Proposed thresholds are starting decisions for the bounded pilot, not contractual service promises.

02. Ownership and component coverage

Each operational fact needs an accountable owner, source and decision. The following coverage map deliberately separates observability from authority. A dashboard never becomes permission to dispatch, modify grants, settle money or re-enable a receiver.

ComponentBoundaryEvidence requiredAccountable functionRunbooks
User interface / critical journeysJ1 service onboarding; J2 outcome repair; J3 reviewed change.Completion cohort, assistance, comprehension, active/handoff time, stale view and unknown acknowledgement.Product/accessibility leadSOP-31 SOP-39
TLS edge / ASGIConfigured serving boundary, independent of the browser.Status classes, latency, inflight, payload size, TLS expiry, denial categories and edge overhead.Platform SRESOP-03 SOP-27
Identity / bindingsReal local or optional OIDC policy; current grants.Sign-in, step-up, provider errors, expiry and revocation outcome; no email/key metric labels.Identity/securitySOP-14 SOP-15
Tenant / workspace scopeApplication authorization; no SQLite RLS.Negative cross-scope probes, scoped exports and verified isolation violations.Backend/securitySOP-13 SOP-34
Service / change controlDraft, review, activation and observed health are separate.Version conflicts, pending age, review content binding, drift and qualification age.Service/release ownerSOP-16 SOP-32
Admission / financeTenant allowance with durable held and accrued amounts.Ledger invariants, available authority, open Unknown exposure, overrun and reconciliation age.Finance/backendSOP-09 SOP-10 SOP-11
Queue / workerDurable claim then execution receipt.Queue depth/age, dispatch age, heartbeat, authority recheck and cancellation race.SRESOP-04 SOP-06 SOP-08
Adapter / providerCandidate is unconnected; R14 execution synthetic.Attempt duration, classification, usage completeness, exact price/contract and unknown outcome.Integration ownerSOP-07 SOP-12
Stream / tools / cachesFuture or separately qualified surface.First-token/gap, terminal state, tool authority and cache isolation; no fabricated series.Integration/securitySOP-07 SOP-13
SQLite / local diskOne server, explicit transactions and DELETE journaling.Bytes, lock waits, write failures, integrity, backup and restore evidence.Storage ownerSOP-17 SOP-18 SOP-19 SOP-20 SOP-21
Observer / casesThree fixed rules; periodic atomic sweep.Checkpoint age, lag, retained runs, observed condition and human ownership.OperationsSOP-22 SOP-23
Outbox / sender / receiverOpt-in receiver; bounded delivery and recovery.Per-state backlog, due lag, signature/TLS preflight, attempts, expiry and uncertainty.Integration ownerSOP-24 SOP-25 SOP-27
Audit / evidenceLocal append-only application records; privileged DB can modify underlying storage.Export lag, missing joins, custody/access, retention and integrity verification.Security/complianceSOP-26 SOP-35
Collection / metrics / logs / tracesProposed independent monitoring plane.Drops, queue saturation, schema errors, freshness, clock error and expected inventory.ObservabilitySOP-02
Optional PostgreSQL / RedisSeparate selected LiteLLM topology only.Pool and transaction capacity, replication, coordination eviction, persistence and recovery.DBA/platformSOP-29 SOP-30
Release / on-call organizationExact artifact and approval responsibility.Required gates, runtime-policy match, ownership coverage, incidents and review cadence.Release/incident ownerSOP-01 SOP-32 SOP-36 SOP-37

Roles are functions to assign to real people before a pilot. No synthetic persona is an on-call assignment. The incident commander coordinates; service owners accept workload risk; finance owns liability interpretation; security owns containment for identity and data exposure; SRE owns serving and telemetry; the integration owner owns receiver/provider contracts. Expansive recovery follows the current approval path, not a pager acknowledgement.

03. Observation architecture and trust boundaries

Authoritative sourcesIdentity • contracts • requests • journal • cases • outbox
read / committed event
Bounded collectionHealth probes • snapshot reader • transition instrumentation • safe access parser
validated, scoped, buffered
Independent monitoringPrometheus rules • logs • traces • out-of-band paging
authorized investigation
Human decisionDiagnose • contain • review • verify • close

The first boundary is between a record of authority and its observational projection. PostgreSQL/SQLite rows or a durable command receipt determine state; Prometheus samples, logs and traces explain it. Loss of a metric must never release a reservation. A sampled trace cannot be the sole audit trail. A late telemetry event cannot retroactively establish that a client received output.

For current-state gauges, collect a consistent bounded snapshot and attach the actual observation timestamp. For event counters, increment at the committed transition or consume a durable outbox with a checkpoint. Do not count the number of retained rows as a monotonic event total, and do not repeatedly add the same historical rows after exporter restart. R14 has no general telemetry outbox; implementing one requires a design and retention decision.

Proposed collection cadence is 15-second scrape and 30-second evaluation for ordinary health/queue gauges. Ledger consistency checks run on a bounded schedule and after recovery, not as a full-table scan every scrape. Expensive integrity scans use a controlled backup. Build source-specific freshness into every panel: a live collector may be exporting an old source snapshot.

Independent failure detection

Expected component inventory and the receiver used for critical alerts must not depend exclusively on the component being monitored. The supplied Alertmanager skeleton has no delivery integrations and must not be mistaken for working paging.

Buffering needs finite queue capacity, bounded retries, disk controls and explicit drop counters. A queue improves resilience but can fill or fail; monitor its exporter as well as the application. Collector persistence and delivery guarantees require their own qualification. EXT-09 SRC-04 SRC-05

OTel is the proposed cross-signal model, not an installed R14 feature. Use trace IDs for correlation and immutable domain IDs for authority. Keep authentication credentials and user-supplied baggage out of metric labels and propagation. The normalized JSON schema in this package is a local interchange contract with an explicit mapping to OTel, not a claim to be native OTLP JSON. EXT-05

04. State semantics operators must not conflate

AxisMeaningSafe operator interpretation
Saved / approved / activated / observedStored intent, reviewed intent, effective configuration and runtime evidence.Approval does not activate; activation does not prove health.
Queued / Dispatching / CompletedAdmitted waiting work, durable dispatch intent and a recorded terminal mock response.Cancel only before dispatch wins the transaction. Completion evidence does not prove human receipt.
Unknown / ReconciledUnresolved outcome and later financial resolution.Keep exposure; reconciliation may retain response_observed=0 and unknown attempt-level cost.
Estimated / held / accrued / invoicedPrediction, authorized exposure, known liability and external billing evidence.Never add child budgets or call an invoice estimate a settled bill.
Active / Clear / Suppressed conditionLatest source evaluation of an operational rule.Suppressed during maintenance does not mean the source is fixed.
Open / Acknowledged / Resolved caseHuman investigation workflow.Acknowledge ownership independently; resolve only after actual clear conditions and explicit review.
Pending / Dispatching / Accepted deliveryOutbox waiting, possible network send and HTTP 2xx receipt.Acceptance is not reading, completion of a downstream action or case resolution.
Fresh / stale / missing evidenceTemporal quality of the observation.Show Unknown and last observation time. Do not paint a stale green result as current.
Operating modeNew executionQueued workNotificationsInvestigation
ServingSubject to all existing controlsEligible dispatch permittedActive routes may deliverAvailable while control storage is healthy
DrainingRejected before new reservationEligible work can finishActive routes may deliverAvailable
PausedRejected before new reservationFrozen; holds remainActive routes may still deliverAvailable
RecoveryRejectedQuarantined; interrupted work remains unknownDisabled/quarantined until deliberately reviewedRead/repair within restoration procedure

The maintenance state machine is installation-wide, not a service-access grant. A successful resume does not reactivate a paused service, restore revoked credentials, expand a budget or re-enable a quarantined route. SRC-02 SRC-03 SRC-05

05. SLIs, SLO proposals and error-budget policy

Start from the operator and developer outcome. Count an admitted logical intent exactly once in a deadline assessment cohort. A replay is not a new eligible request; retries are separate attempts underneath it. Correct policy denials are excluded from inference availability but remain visible as UX and security signals. Provider errors, malformed usage and unresolved execution that misses the approved deadline are bad outcomes. Once classified at its deadline, a late success does not erase that missed-deadline event.

For an implementation with queued work, classify at completion or the contracted deadline so permanently queued/Unknown work cannot disappear from the denominator. An admission/API availability SLI separately catches requests never admitted due to platform outage. Maintain both outcomes in counters from initialization. A zero denominator displays No eligible traffic; a missing series displays Unknown. Neither is a 100% success claim.

SLIProposed starting objectiveQualification / exclusion rules
Control requests99.9% valid authorized control operations within approved response deadline over 30 days.Exclude expected authorization/validation rejections, not storage faults. Decide deadline using measured workload.
Logical execution99.9% eligible intents achieve the declared terminal contract within deadline over 30 days.Deadline per approved service. Unknown at deadline is bad. Keep synthetic traffic separate.
Latencyp95 control ≤1 second; nonstreaming execution target chosen per workload.Illustrative only. Use full edge timing and histogram boundary matching target; proxy time alone misses pre-handler delay.
Financial consistencyZero unexplained ledger/reservation mismatches.Safety invariant, not a percentage error budget that authorizes losing money.
Revocation and isolationZero verified post-revocation unauthorized dispatch or cross-scope disclosures.Safety invariants. Explicit bounded propagation contract for any future cache.
Operational detectionSignal-source evidence fresh within R14 30-second contract; external detection budget approved separately.R14 sweeps every 10 seconds; scraper/evaluation/for/delivery delays add to alert arrival.
Notification timelinessPilot: 99% eligible events accepted within five minutes, separately track 24-hour expiry.Proposed observer metric; 2xx is receiver acceptance only. Disabled routes excluded visibly, not silently dropped.
User journeysBaseline correct completion, assistance and comprehension for J1/J2/J3 before targets.No invented percentages without representative sessions. Active time and waiting for approval remain separate.
RecoveryPropose daily backup and monthly drill; select RPO/RTO from business loss and exposure tolerances.No achieved RPO/RTO claim. Same-host drill cannot prove region loss or recover liabilities incurred after backup.

The included example assumes a 99.9% availability objective over 30 days. Allowed error fraction is 0.001; burn rate equals observed bad fraction divided by 0.001. The fast pair is 1h/5m at burn 14.4 (bad fraction 0.0144); the slow pair is 6h/30m at burn 6 (0.006). They are templates requiring eligible-count and traffic-quality gates. A separate low-volume rule handles three bad events in fifteen minutes when volume is too small for the primary ratio. EXT-04

Proposed error-budget response: fast burn pages the service owner and freezes nonessential risky releases; slow burn opens a timed engineering investigation; repeated exhaustion requires a reliability plan. Security, financial integrity and missing telemetry are never traded against an availability budget. Planned maintenance is included unless the approved contract explicitly excludes it. Dashboard exclusions, alert silences and user communications must reference the same reviewed maintenance window.

06. Metric semantics, cardinality and aggregation

The canonical metric registry is a proposed emission API. Every definition has a type, unit, dimension contract, source, owner, missing/reset behavior and links to dependent alarms or queries. “Existing evidence; exporter required” means a fact can be derived from preserved code or records; it does not mean R14 exports that metric today. Future streaming/cache/tool entries stay disabled until those surfaces are implemented and qualified.

RuleOperational consequence
Counters are cumulative eventsUse rate/increase over a window; process restart handling belongs to the monitoring system. Never count retained rows as lifetime events.
Gauges represent a point-in-time statePublish a successful snapshot and its freshness. Emit zero for a proven empty result only.
Histogram buckets share one semantic clockAggregate buckets then compute quantiles. Do not average p95s. Do not infer end-to-end latency by summing independently sampled p95s.
Money uses explicit basisR14 _u fields are integer USD micro-units. Do not mix currency, synthetic prices, discounts or tax without a versioned conversion/reconciliation policy.
Dimensions are boundedenv/cell are deployment-controlled. mode/state/reason/provider aliases come from a reviewed finite vocabulary.
Identity stays outside global metricsNo request IDs, raw URLs, emails, IPs, keys, prompts, user agents or unbounded tenant labels. Authorized drill-down belongs in the data plane for administration.
Hierarchy is not additiveA tenant allowance and its child limits are not independent funds. Aggregate disjoint resources or show separate scopes.
Sampling is not accountingNever sample ledger/audit transitions needed for correctness. Trace/log sampling is separate and cannot create a complete denominator.

Classic histogram buckets are supplied for interoperable queries and fixtures; each has an implicit +Inf bucket, sum and count. Native histograms are another possible implementation, not automatically interchangeable with the supplied _bucket queries. Select and test one contract per family. EXT-01 EXT-18

Proposed cardinality budget: begin with an approved ceiling such as 50,000 active series per execution cell, then measure actual cost and adjust under review. This is a planning input, not a known limit. A classic histogram with B finite buckets generates B+3 series per label combination before optional creation samples; with 14 finite buckets, 6 route classes and 3 cells that is 306 series for one family. Multiplying by user or request identity rapidly destroys this bound. Account for replicas, status/outcome combinations and rollout overlap, not merely the number of metric names.

Reduce dimensions at the producer or through a deliberate aggregation layer. Blindly dropping distinguishing labels from raw samples can create colliding series; downstream dashboard grouping does not remove sensitive labels already ingested. Explicitly preserve a sample’s environment and cell during federation. Require fixture zero states, missing-state tests and future-timestamp tests before approving a panel.

Proposed finite histogram boundaries: 0.005, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 60, 120; implicit +Inf.

Proposed classic histogram finite boundaries plus implicit +Inf, _sum and _count. Include the approved SLO boundary before rollout. Never average quantiles or precomputed percentiles. Native histograms are a separate compatibility choice.

Sources: EXT-01

Dependent alarms: No direct threshold. Diagnostic/qualification metric.

08. LiteLLM native inventory and integration mapping

The pinned fork declares 85 names in DEFINED_PROMETHEUS_METRICS. This section enumerates every member of that inspected declaration. It also lists 27 supplemental family names from live documentation, including parameterized service-call families. A declaration is not proof that every handler emits a series, that every edition enables it, or that no additional metrics exist elsewhere. SRC-13

For any selected deployment, capture its authorized exposition without copying bearer credentials into history, inventory #TYPE and sample/label names, compare against the pinned catalog, and attach the source commit, wheel/container digest, configuration digest and actual scrape time. The supplied inventory tool processes a local capture and emits names only; it intentionally discards label values, sample values, HELP prose and exemplars. It is an inventory utility, not a complete Prometheus parser.

Safe native collection

Keep the metrics endpoint private and authenticated. Qualify worker aggregation, allowlisted labels, and delayed or sparse budget/usage fields for the actual topology. Native provider prompt-cache usage and gateway response-cache usage are different signals. EXT-14

Mapping is semantic, not a string replacement. Proxy logical requests map to a logical-demand view; deployment attempts map to retry/cost analysis. A native spend counter maps to reported usage cost, not Aperture reserved exposure or an invoice. The source’s project/consumer labels and zero-cost/capture-rate families are review targets, not automatic authorization or loss detection. Retired/deprecated request metrics must not be added to replacement counters. Latency clocks need explicit alignment to ingress, queue, provider and terminal response. Request IDs and x-litellm-call-id require a proven integration mapping; do not assume identical identity.

Qualification
Confirm emission path, type, units, complete label set, edition/callback gate, zero/absence semantics, reset behavior and failure-path coverage before use.

09. Alarm lifecycle, severity and maintenance

Alerts should identify a decision that somebody can make. The 48 rules are proposed monitoring contracts, each with an owner, condition, persistence, source dependency, runbook and recovery expectation. They are not installed in R14. Critical safety alarms remain independent from normal availability thresholds.

ClassProposed handlingNever do
Critical integrity / securityImmediate external notification; incident declaration; smallest safe containment.Wait for an availability burn threshold or silence because maintenance exists.
Critical availability / observationPage when freshness and scope support the conclusion; show affected boundary and missing evidence.Use the affected sender as the only notification route.
Warning operational / expiryTimed ownership and investigation; escalate by age and exposure.Produce repeated tickets for the same unchanged condition.
Product / qualification blockerAssign to a product/release owner; block promotion when relevant.Treat a known accessibility defect as a noisy 24-hour emergency page by default.
Resolved metric conditionVerify at least two fresh evaluations and the user/financial state, then close the human incident explicitly.Close a financial hold or operational case because an alert disappeared.

Evaluate a rule only with its required source contract. Missing series can cause PromQL to return nothing; no vector is not proof of health. AL-01/AL-02 independently detect missing observation. The supplied rule group does not globally gate every alarm on one core timestamp; each component needs its own expected inventory and freshness evidence. Dashboards must show stale rather than silently resolve old safety claims.

Maintenance silences need an owner, approved scope, start/end, reason and automatic expiry. Eligibility is metadata only; this package does not create a runtime maintenance-inhibition service. Never silence cross-scope exposure, financial mismatches, verified telemetry leakage or monitoring loss merely to keep a maintenance dashboard green. Group related alarms by env/cell and root cause; inhibition should hide redundant symptoms, not independent safety failures. EXT-02 EXT-08

Alertmanager delivery intentionally unconfigured

The routing skeleton contains empty receiver definitions and example runbook hosts. This is safe for review but not safe to deploy as a functioning paging setup: it would provide no actual external notification. OG-10 requires receiver ownership, secret handling, delivery tests and an independent fallback route.

10. Full alarm register and recording rules

Downloads: 48 alert rules, nine recording rules, five native expression fixtures, unconfigured routing skeleton. All templates require the metrics declared in this document; there is no R14 exporter installing them.

AL-01 · AlarmApertureTelemetryMissingcritical · for 2m · SOP-02
PromQL template
aperture_telemetry_component_expected == 1 unless on(env,cell,component) ((time() - aperture_telemetry_snapshot_timestamp_seconds >= 0) and (time() - aperture_telemetry_snapshot_timestamp_seconds < 60))
Meaning
Expected exporter snapshot missing, future-dated or stale. Inventory must be independent of failed component.
Severity
critical
Owner
observability
Pending duration
2m
Dependencies
independent inventory + timestamps
Maintenance
never
Data quality
Dashboard must show unavailable/unknown when source absent. AL-01 and AL-02 remain active independently; no successful inference from silent rules.
Recovery
Fresh source evidence below threshold for two evaluations, then explicit incident-owner closure; alert resolution is not financial settlement.
Status
Proposed alarm contract; not enabled in R14

Runbook: SOP-02 · Diagnostics: Use linked SOP and source-specific dashboards.

11. Log structures, event taxonomy and redaction

R14’s ASGI logger emits six fields. The development HTTP server intentionally suppresses its normal access logger. Do not advertise identical coverage between those transports. ASGI duration includes work from app entry, including its local semaphore wait, but cannot observe earlier load-balancer or pre-ASGI scheduling delay. SRC-06 SRC-07

Actual R14 access shape; synthetic sample
{
  "request_id": "9e17ee0398764229a1a9be444faa0112",
  "route_class": "api",
  "method": "POST",
  "status": 503,
  "code": "ADMISSION_MAINTENANCE",
  "duration_ms": 6.3
}

The access request_id is generated at the edge. It is not automatically the durable request row ID or a W3C trace ID. Native JSON has no source timestamp; collection must preserve observed time separately rather than invent an origin time. The existing shape is validated in r14-access.schema.json.

The proposed aperture.ops.v1 envelope separates operational, security, financial, audit and UX records. Map occurred_at to origin time and observed_at to collection time, severity to the appropriate OTel fields, and resource identity to service/version/environment/cell. If an original field is absent, retain null/absence. Do not manufacture event success or cost. EXT-05

Proposed normalized event; synthetic example
{
  "schema_version": "aperture.ops.v1",
  "event_id": "example-event-001",
  "occurred_at": "2026-10-21T09:00:00Z",
  "observed_at": "2026-10-21T09:00:01Z",
  "severity_text": "WARN",
  "severity_number": 13,
  "event_name": "execution.unknown",
  "record_class": "operational",
  "resource": {
    "service_name": "aperture",
    "version": "14-fixture",
    "environment": "test",
    "cell": "local"
  },
  "scope_kind": "workspace",
  "tenant_ref": "tenant-demo",
  "workspace_ref": "workspace-demo",
  "classification": "Restricted",
  "content_captured": false,
  "synthetic": true,
  "outcome": "unknown",
  "request_id": "request-demo",
  "amount_micro_usd": null,
  "cost_basis": "unknown"
}
Record classEvent families / suggested triggersRequired interpretation
Operationalrequest.admitted/dispatch_started/completed/unknown; worker.failed; signal.transition; notification.transition.One domain transition per event; link durable IDs; observation-only events cannot change authority.
Securityauth.denied; identity.revoked; scope.violation; telemetry.redaction_failed.Safe reason enums, bounded metadata; confirmed violation distinct from correctly denied attempt.
Auditchange.proposed/approved/activated; credentials.revoked; operating_state.changed; authorized export.Actor reference, resource and revision; never token, session cookie, full grant payload or unrestricted detail JSON.
Financialreservation.held/released; cost.observed; settlement.recorded; ledger.mismatch.Currency basis, price revision, known amount or null, and independent held/accrued deltas.
UXjourney.started/assisted/completed; command.ack_unknown; view.stale.Pseudonymous subject/session where genuinely needed under policy; no keypress recording or prompt content.

Use a schema allowlist before serialization, not only regex redaction afterward. Prohibit Authorization/Cookie/Set-Cookie values, passwords, CSRF/OIDC codes, signing material, raw idempotency keys, prompt/response bodies and remote exception bodies. Restrict free-text notes to their authorized application records. Detect schema failures using finite safe reason codes; do not log the rejected raw record. EXT-17

12. Traces, correlation and causal timelines

A request explanation needs several identities with different lifetimes. Preserve them separately so a retried HTTP call, one logical command, an execution attempt, a billing observation and a notification are not collapsed into one misleading request count.

IdentityOwner and lifetimeSafe correlation
edge_request_idOne HTTP handling attempt; currently access request_id.Join to a logical command only after an explicit server-generated mapping exists.
logical_request_idOne authorized execution intent persisted before worker processing.Stable across receipt replay; never reconstruct from raw caller idempotency key in logs.
attempt_idOne claimed dispatch.Provider request IDs attach here; a failed-primary attempt still may incur liability.
trace_id / span_idObservational propagation; may be missing or sampled.Link asynchronous worker spans to admission rather than inventing synchronous parent duration.
service / contract / price revisionsApproved execution and accounting context.Attach bounded fingerprints in logs; avoid unbounded revision labels across all metrics.
observation / settlement IDsIndependent cost evidence and financial decision.A settlement links to request and observation, not a claim of response delivery.
case occurrence / notification event IDHuman incident recurrence and external delivery identity.Retries retain event identity and body; receipt ID is not the case state.

Proposed spans: edge.accept → authority.evaluate → financial.reserve → queue.persist; worker.claim links to admission; provider.attempt records dispatch intent and terminal evidence; financial.record follows known usage; telemetry/export and notification spans are independent. Record status and safe category rather than payload. Mark whether a failure occurred before dispatch, after durable dispatch intent, after upstream response or after local receipt commit.

Security and financial journals must remain complete even when traces are sampled. For ordinary traces, start with a reviewed head-sampling rate and separately qualify error/latency retention. Tail sampling needs buffers and can lose observations when they overflow. Do not base billing, access review or a count-based SLI solely on sampled traces. In incident views, display gaps and timestamp uncertainty, not a continuous fictional waterfall.

13. Query cookbook: metrics, logs and authoritative records

This cookbook contains 40 SQLite diagnostics, 24 PromQL recipes, 30 LogQL recipes and six PostgreSQL templates. Actual R14 diagnostics execute on a consistent protected snapshot. PostgreSQL and Loki examples remain templates; exact parser and execution evidence is separately reported. SQL parameters and dashboard variables are inputs to a trusted tool, not authorization controls.

Read-only does not mean unprivileged

SQLite file possession is installation-level access. Only authorized operators may create and query snapshots. A tenant/workspace WHERE clause is not row-level security. Use the application’s scope enforcement for user-facing dashboards and exports. Never expose this diagnostic tool through an unprotected web endpoint.

Run from extracted consolidated package root; example IDs are synthetic
python operations/tools/query_snapshot.py /private/r14-snapshot.sqlite3 SQL-06 \
  --operator --tenant harbor --out /private/new-ledger-diagnostic.json

python operations/tools/query_snapshot.py /private/r14-snapshot.sqlite3 SQL-04 \
  --tenant harbor --workspace ws-briefs --out /private/new-unknown-diagnostic.json

The runner accepts catalogued query IDs only, opens mode=ro, enables query_only, requires explicit scope, enforces a progress timeout and writes a new 0600 output file. It reports truncation. It does not change the database or certify current live state. Queries aggregate full scoped records before limiting detail. Invoice data, real provider logs and raw secrets are not present in the supplied fixture.

LogQL variables need validated/escaped substitution by the authorized dashboard. Tenant filtering inside LogQL is not a Loki tenancy policy. R14-access queries need ingestion labels supplied by the collector. Normalized-event queries cannot work until ops_v1 records exist. Query filters must surface parser errors separately rather than hiding them as a healthy absence.

Scope

Parameters
Interpretation
Mode is installation-wide. Paused stops new inference and queued dispatch, not notification egress.

SQL-06 · QueryTenant ledger consistencySQLite · tenant
SQLite
WITH j AS (SELECT tenant,SUM(held_delta) held,SUM(accrued_delta) accrued FROM journal WHERE tenant=:tenant GROUP BY tenant),
r AS (SELECT tenant,SUM(CASE WHEN state IN('Queued','Dispatching','Unknown') THEN reserved_u ELSE 0 END) held,SUM(COALESCE(cost_u,0)) accrued FROM requests WHERE tenant=:tenant GROUP BY tenant)
SELECT t.id,t.held_u,t.accrued_u,COALESCE(j.held,0) journal_held,COALESCE(j.accrued,0) journal_accrued,COALESCE(r.held,0) request_held,COALESCE(r.accrued,0) request_accrued,
 t.held_u-COALESCE(j.held,0) held_journal_delta,t.accrued_u-COALESCE(j.accrued,0) accrued_journal_delta,
 t.held_u-COALESCE(r.held,0) held_request_delta,t.accrued_u-COALESCE(r.accrued,0) accrued_request_delta
FROM tenants t LEFT JOIN j ON j.tenant=t.id LEFT JOIN r ON r.tenant=t.id WHERE t.id=:tenant
Scope
tenant
Parameters
tenant
Interpretation
Expected deltas are zero. Aggregate independent relations before joining to avoid multiplication. COALESCE here sums known accrued cost, not an estimate of unknown liability.
Validation boundary
Executed on actual preserved R14 schema with synthetic scoped records; no production database accessed.

SQL-12 · QueryRequest/job consistencySQLite · workspace
SQLite
SELECT r.id,r.state request_state,j.state job_state FROM requests r LEFT JOIN jobs j ON j.request=r.id
WHERE r.tenant=:tenant AND r.workspace=:workspace AND
((r.state='Queued' AND (j.state IS NULL OR j.state!='Queued')) OR (r.state='Dispatching' AND (j.state IS NULL OR j.state!='Dispatching')) OR (r.state='Unknown' AND (j.state IS NULL OR j.state!='Unknown')) OR (r.state IN('Completed','Not dispatched','Declined') AND (j.state IS NULL OR j.state!='Done')) OR (r.state='Reconciled' AND (j.state IS NULL OR j.state NOT IN('Unknown','Done'))) OR (r.state='Denied' AND j.id IS NOT NULL)) LIMIT 200
Scope
workspace
Parameters
tenant; workspace
Interpretation
Expected empty. R14 cancellation persists Not dispatched, not a new Cancelled request state.
Validation boundary
Executed on actual preserved R14 schema with synthetic scoped records; no production database accessed.

SQL-15 · QueryStale reviewed base versionsSQLite · workspace
SQLite
SELECT c.id,c.kind,c.status,c.base_version,CASE WHEN c.kind='contract' THEN s.version ELSE w.version END current_version
FROM changes c LEFT JOIN services s ON c.kind='contract' AND s.id=c.resource LEFT JOIN workloads w ON c.kind='workload' AND w.id=c.resource

14. Dashboard specifications and drill-down

The dashboard set is organized by decisions, not database tables. Every panel displays scope, source version, observation age, complete versus truncated coverage, and whether data is synthetic. The specifications below do not pretend to be provisioned Grafana dashboards. They define the panels and evidence paths an implementation must satisfy.

Access
Tenant-authorized service projection; fleet totals restricted.

Proposed dashboard contract; data pipeline and access policy must be implemented.

A user should move from service health to request explanation, financial timeline or owned case without losing scope. Cross-service views remain privileged and cannot disclose parent financial totals to a workspace-only developer. Empty states say whether there is no data, no access, no enabled feature or a stale source. The safest default is a task-oriented summary plus a precise next action, with raw queries available only to appropriate operators.

15. Multi-tenancy, privacy, evidence custody and retention

Operations has a separate threat surface: broad database read access, centralized logs, monitoring labels, callback payloads and backups can concentrate information that ordinary users cannot see. Assign separate identities for collection, rule evaluation, incident triage and administration. Scope limits must apply to deep links, exports, cached investigation views and replayed commands—not just navigation menus.

Use privileged aggregate metrics for fleet health and an authorized service for tenant finance or request drill-down. For enterprise Loki, implement real tenant isolation and enforce the tenant header at the trusted ingestion/query boundary; a client-provided label or variable is not enough. Omit customer names and raw user identities from global labels. Protect audit and financial streams more strictly than diagnostic access logs. EXT-07

Data classProposed initial custody/retention policyApproval / caveat
Access and ordinary operational logs14 days searchable, up to 90 days controlled archive if justified.Planning values only. Minimize fields; approve jurisdiction and incident legal hold.
Metrics30 days fine-grained; 13 months approved aggregates when economics justify it.Cardinality and cost review; aggregation must not misrepresent missing source periods.
Traces7 days ordinary, up to 30 days sampled incident traces.Sampling policy and buffer losses explicit; no prompt/response content by default.
Financial and audit evidenceRetention decided by finance/legal and organizational policy; no universal duration asserted.Separate integrity/custody controls. A purge must not erase unresolved liability or required evidence.
Browser UX/researchMinimized pseudonymous operational events; consented study records separate.Withdrawal/export/backup limits disclosed; no participant names in metrics.
BackupsProtected versioned copies with tested restoration and explicit recovery envelope.Deletion/hold obligations apply to copies; live ledger retention is not inferred from backup TTL.
Incident packetsSafe IDs, time range, exact versions, hashes, actions and evidence gaps.Never bearer tokens, raw webhook bodies with private notes, or unrestricted database dumps.

These are proposed design inputs, not legal advice or a compliance certification. Approve them before collecting enterprise data. Logs can contain personally identifiable or sensitive operational metadata even without prompts. External recipients require a data inventory, purpose, deletion obligations and incident contacts. Copying an event to a webhook expands custody; application withdrawal cannot recall an exported copy.

On redaction failure, contain the destination, preserve restricted evidence, scope access, revoke affected secrets where appropriate and follow the organization’s security/privacy response. Do not circulate raw records to prove the defect. Enforce schema allowlists and authorized export tests before adding new fields. EXT-17

16. Financial operations and meaningful optimization

Financial operations has three separate jobs: bound dispatch authority, account for known and unresolved liability, and reconcile against external billing at the granularity the provider actually supplies. An operations dashboard must show held exposure and Unknown age alongside accrued spend. A falling rate-limit counter or reset budget must not be interpreted as cancellation of previously dispatched liability.

R14 independent consistency checks; evaluate disjoint aggregates before joining
available_u = budget_u - accrued_u - held_u
held_u = SUM(journal.held_delta)
held_u = SUM(request.reserved_u WHERE state IN (Queued, Dispatching, Unknown))
accrued_u = SUM(journal.accrued_delta)
accrued_u = SUM(known request.cost_u)

These equations test the implemented reference, not a universal accounting schema. SQL-06 intentionally aggregates journal and requests separately, avoiding Cartesian fanout. A zero mismatch does not prove an external invoice is correct, nor that post-backup dispatches were recovered. Unknown request costs are null. Financial reconciliation can assign a known request total without assigning unknowable input/output costs to an individual attempt. SRC-01 SRC-02 SRC-11

Daily proposed finance review: examine held exposure by age, observation backlog, overruns, stale price qualification, missing usage and aggregate invoice variance. Assign each difference to timing, price version, discounts, cache usage, credits, tax, currency, external charges or genuinely unexplained exposure. Do not force invoices into per-request joins that the provider cannot support.

Optimization uses matched workload cohorts and cost per accepted business outcome. Define acceptance independently of HTTP 200 and include failed attempts, retry cost, guardrail/tool charges, engineering toil and any reviewed attribution of capacity cost. Compare qualified alternatives under the same input/output, region, privacy and quality constraints. R14 synthetic units and fixture quality are never evidence of real savings.

Provider prompt cache, gateway exact/semantic response cache, batching and smaller models have different failure and privacy modes. Reuse is permitted only with authorization and capability qualification. A cache hit may avoid an upstream charge but can still consume platform capacity and require policy checks. Changes derived from an optimization recommendation go through the same review and activation model; recommendations never expand scope automatically.

17. Automation boundaries and operational commands

Automation should collect evidence, find drift, prepare proposals and execute already-authorized narrow procedures. It should not infer permission from a health alert. Distinguish read-only diagnostics, restrictive containment and expansive restoration. All potentially expansive actions need current authority, idempotency, a reviewed target state and an explicit evidence trail.

Operation classExamplesRequired behavior
ObservationProbe, permitted SQL snapshot diagnostic, exposition inventory, case collection.No source writes; bounded size/time; safe metadata; freshness and scope shown.
Restrictive containmentPause execution, disable a receiver, revoke a binding, suspend rollout.Least necessary scope; reason/reference; preserves liabilities and history.
Expansive recoveryResume, reactivate a service/grant/route, retry an external delivery.Current-state review; exact plan/version; acknowledgement where required; no automatic widening.
Financial decisionRecord an observation, settle Unknown exposure, acknowledge an overrun.Separate evidence and settlement commands. Do not imply two independent finance actors unless policy actually requires it.
Offline maintenanceUpgrade, identity binding changes, snapshot restoration.Follow stopped-process prerequisites and exclusive destination rules; no automatic schema repair on serving.

The runbook commands use current R14 CLI syntax. Global --db precedes the subcommand. Backups take a positional destination. Restoration takes positional source and new destination; never target the live database. Local trusted-operator references are attestations, not cryptographically verified dual approval. Sensitive console decisions reauthenticate where required and never automatically retry after verification. SRC-08

Executable command shapes, not automatic instructions to apply to a live installation
python server.py --db "$DB" ops-status
python server.py --db "$DB" signals-status
python server.py --db "$DB" verify

# Restrictive control. Replace references with approved, nonsecret records.
python server.py --db "$DB" ops-pause \
  --reason "Investigate the current execution incident" \
  --reference "INCIDENT-APPROVED-REFERENCE"

# Separate egress containment when required.
python server.py --db "$DB" notification-route-disable "$ROUTE_ID" \
  --reference "INCIDENT-EGRESS-REFERENCE"

Do not implement autonomous SQL financial repairs, blanket role grants, forced retries after unknown execution, unrestricted receiver URLs, TLS bypasses or repeated resume attempts. The report supplies decisions and safe command shapes; operator context and exact evidence determine whether they apply.

18. Capacity, storage, backup and disaster recovery

R14 is a local single-server reference. Horizontal scaling by starting a second writer is not an accepted capacity plan. Measure the selected workload’s queue arrival/service rates, long transactions, backup impact, maximum connection/request bodies, process memory and evidence growth. Alert on increasing queue age and rejection outcomes rather than relying on CPU alone. Scale the architecture only after defining how authority, dispatch and reconciliation remain correct.

A proposed minimum capacity exercise includes steady-state eligible traffic, bursts, slow provider responses, fivefold callback latency, persistent Unknown outcomes, large audit volumes, an overdue observer and a stalled receiver. Concurrency, sustainable throughput and recovery targets must be measured, not copied from a vendor’s benchmark. Separate request admission fairness from spend allowance; one does not guarantee the other.

Use the SQLite backup API or the current verified backup command for a consistent copy; do not copy a live database file without accounting for its journaling and transactions. Validate integrity on the copied database, then test restoration in quarantine. Keep protected output files outside public/static roots. EXT-10 EXT-11 SRC-01

Actual CLI signatures; choose nonexisting destinations
python server.py --db "$DB" backup /private/new-r14-backup.sqlite3
python server.py --db "$DB" recovery-drill --out /private/new-recovery-evidence.json

# For a separately reviewed restoration, with the source server stopped:
python server.py restore /private/approved-backup.sqlite3 /private/new-recovery.sqlite3
python server.py --db /private/new-recovery.sqlite3 verify

Restoration revokes inherited sessions, bindings and workload credentials, pauses services and budgets, quarantines execution and egress, and preserves evidence for review. A successful local drill proves those observed local properties. It does not reconstruct requests dispatched after the backup, prove another host/region’s recovery, or establish a measured RPO/RTO. Capture the outstanding exposure and independent provider/receiver evidence before any expansive recovery. SRC-03 SRC-08

For a selected LiteLLM deployment, qualify PostgreSQL backup/PITR, connection roles, migrations and replication separately. Redis used for financial/rate-limit coordination requires a different availability and eviction policy from disposable response cache. Read-only INFO and bounded exporter metrics are preferable to KEYS, MONITOR or production key dumping. The optional queries here are investigation starting points, not permission to cancel transactions or modify persistence. EXT-12 EXT-13

19. User experience as an operational concern

The operational interface should answer four questions first: What is affected? What do we know? What remains unknown? What can my current role safely do? Place source age and scope alongside every conclusion. Maintain a stable case/request link across pages, preserve the selected tenant/workspace, and distinguish loading, denied, empty, stale and unavailable states.

For J1, observe whether a service owner can prepare a valid draft, reach independent review, activate it and complete the first qualified request. For J2, measure whether a developer can understand a denial or Unknown outcome and choose a permitted next step. For J3, verify that a reviewer can explain the exact difference and tell approval, activation and health apart. Count only genuinely matched cohorts; do not divide this week’s completions by a different cohort’s starts.

Record active task time separately from waiting for review or infrastructure. Comprehension requires an observed explanation, not a button click. Assistance, interruption, abandonment and missing observation stay visible. Synthetic rehearsals do not enter participant success statistics. Known accessibility barriers block acceptance of the affected critical task even when the backend is healthy.

A lost acknowledgement has a special UX contract: keep the original logical key, expose a safe receipt-recovery action, and reauthorize before replay. Do not tell the user that reauthentication proves whether a preceding command committed. A retry button must not mint a fresh charge or restore revoked permissions. Use safe reason codes with actionable ownership and avoid raw server stack traces. SOP-39

Dashboards and alarms also consume human attention. Prefer one owned incident with correlated symptoms to many duplicate pages. Keep action controls close to evidence but require explicit review for widening authority. Preserve keyboard focus, meaningful labels, responsive tables and reduced-motion preferences. Native browser, screen-reader and representative-user testing remain separate qualification obligations.

20. Incident policy, escalation and communications

PriorityIllustrative triggerProposed response agreementClosure evidence
P1Verified disclosure/unauthorized dispatch, financial mismatch, or broad failure of a critical approved service.Named commander immediately; target acknowledgement ≤5 minutes and initial stakeholder statement ≤15 minutes.Threat contained, invariants/current authorization verified, user impact understood and outstanding liabilities assigned.
P2Bounded service degradation, unowned financial hold, failing approved receiver or degraded observation.Target acknowledgement ≤15 minutes; update at least every 30 minutes while material impact persists.Fresh evidence below threshold, root impact addressed and permanent fix/owner recorded.
P3Expiry warning, drift without exposure, cost opportunity or nonblocking UX defect.Business-hours owner and due date; promotion blocked where the defect affects a required gate.Evidence-backed correction or an explicit risk decision with expiry.

Response times are proposed operating agreements, not measured service guarantees. Names, coverage hours, contact paths, authority and escalation timers must be assigned before a pilot. Each handoff includes scope, current mode, evidence freshness, unresolved exposure, actions already taken, next safe decision and explicit owner acceptance. Use an external communication path if Aperture or its sender is impaired.

Status updates describe observed impact and uncertainty without exposing tenant identities to other tenants. Record UTC times and local display offsets. Do not promise recovery estimates without evidence. Closure is a human decision after source verification; disappearing alerts, stopped traffic or a receiver’s 202 response do not close the incident. A post-incident review separates trigger, contributing factors, missing controls and action ownership.

21. Full standard operating procedure register

Forty procedures cover detection, investigation, containment, recovery, reconciliation, maintenance and product quality. They are written for the actual R14 reference where commands exist, and explicitly identify optional/proposed integrations. Operators must use current permissions and source evidence. None authorizes direct journal editing, unsafe transport bypass or silent privilege expansion.

SOP-01 · SOPDeclare, coordinate and hand off an incidentAll baselines
Owner
Incident commander
Applies to
All baselines
Trigger
A critical alert, confirmed isolation/integrity failure or user-blocking critical journey.
Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Confirm alert freshness, affected environment/cell and source version. Determine control, execution, identity, financial, evidence and delivery impacts independently.
  2. Establish incident commander, technical lead, communications lead and financial/security lead when relevant. Use an external channel if Aperture is affected.
  3. Classify P1 immediately for verified disclosure, unauthorized authority, ledger mismatch or widespread unavailable critical tasks. P2 is bounded degradation without integrity loss.

Procedure

  1. Open an incident record with initial blast radius and an explicit unknowns list.
  2. Attach safe metric/query references; preserve original timestamps and hashes. Declare who can approve expansive recovery.
  3. Give an initial impact statement and update cadence. Proposed targets are P1 acknowledgement within 5 minutes and updates every 15 minutes, subject to accepted staffing.
  4. Delegate one hypothesis per investigator. Keep a shared UTC timeline of observations and actions, not an unreviewed command stream.
  5. Handoff explicitly includes current mode, queued/dispatching/unknown counts, holds, active silences, pending changes and receiver uncertainty.

Verification / exit checks: Incoming owner repeats the outstanding risks and next safe actions; monitoring and communication channels both work.

Containment: Use the smallest reversible restriction consistent with current authority. Do not broaden access, bypass budget reservation or disable TLS to restore a green dashboard.

Rollback: Withdraw a mistaken incident severity only with recorded rationale; preserve events and state changes.

Escalation: Page security for suspected disclosure or compromised identity, FinOps for financial uncertainty, and storage owner for state corruption. Never rely only on R14 webhook delivery.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

Closure: Incident owner confirms outcome against fresh source evidence and updates customer-facing status. A cleared alarm, successful probe or HTTP acknowledgement alone does not close the incident.

Review cadence: Before production enablement, after source/contract change, and at least quarterly. Run on fixtures first.

SOP-02 · SOPRecover monitoring and detect missing evidenceProposed telemetry plane plus current health/SQL
Owner
Observability on-call
Applies to
Proposed telemetry plane plus current health/SQL
Trigger
AL-01/02/39/40 or a stale dashboard despite reported traffic.
Preconditions

Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Check independent expected-target inventory and scrape status. Missing series is not a value of zero.
  2. Compare collection time, source time and query availability; verify clock offset before concluding export delay.
  3. Inspect collector/export queue capacity, dropped-record counters and destination authentication without exporting payloads.

Procedure

  1. Distinguish collection failure, exporter failure, storage/query failure and dashboard filter error.
  2. Restore the affected pipeline with pinned configuration and narrowly scoped credentials. Increase buffers only within measured disk/memory limits.
  3. Run a unique synthetic safe event through collection and query; record source/observed/query timestamps.
  4. Recover queued telemetry with event deduplication. Replay evidence, never execution commands.
  5. Reconcile blind-spot start/end against durable source tables and disclose permanent gaps.

Verification / exit checks: Expected components are present, source timestamps advance, test events are queryable and zero drops persist for two observation windows.

Containment: Preserve authoritative audit/financial state. Do not make critical decisions from stale charts. Use health probes and a bounded protected snapshot as a documented fallback.

Rollback: Revert the collector/configuration change through its deployment path. Retain buffered data securely and do not silently truncate queues.

Escalation: Incident commander if visibility over authorization, financial safety or multiple cells is lost; security if loss might conceal disclosure.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

Closure: Incident owner confirms outcome against fresh source evidence and updates customer-facing status. A cleared alarm, successful probe or HTTP acknowledgement alone does not close the incident.

SOP-04 · SOPRecover a stuck execution queue or workerR14 current; candidate later
Owner
Execution on-call
Applies to
R14 current; candidate later
Trigger
Old queued work, Serving without healthy execution worker or AL-04/05/13.
Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Compare Queued, Dispatching and Unknown inventories with current operating mode. Paused is intentionally frozen.
  2. Inspect complete queue age and job/request correspondence, not recent 200-row UI lists.
  3. Check worker loop evidence, SQLite lock waits and current service/workload/tenant restrictions. An alive loop does not prove progress.

Procedure

  1. Record current source counts and oldest request IDs.
  2. Resolve underlying configuration/storage issue without changing queued request payloads or current grants.
  3. For work no longer wanted, use explicit cancellation before durable dispatch intent (SOP-06).
  4. If controlled restart is necessary, record Dispatching work first; recovery can classify it Unknown. Do not turn it back into Queued manually.
  5. Use fresh exact-state resume plan only after current authority and balances verify.

Verification / exit checks: Eligible queue age decreases, no duplicate attempt appears and financial holds match source requests; unrelated tenants remain isolated.

Containment: Drain to stop new admissions while allowing eligible queued work to finish, or pause when existing queued dispatch itself is unsafe. Do not add a second server process.

Rollback: Pause again if progress or invariants regress. Keep Unknown reservations rather than retrying upstream.

Escalation: Execution and storage owners; FinOps when interrupted work carries unresolved exposure.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

SOP-05 · SOPDrain, pause and resume safelyR14 current
Owner
Trusted installation operator
Applies to
R14 current
Trigger
Planned maintenance or containment requiring installation-wide execution restriction.
Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Use ops-status to establish mode, counts and held exposure. Draining permits eligible queued dispatch; Paused does not.
  2. Understand that notification sender and case observer continue outside Recovery. Disable approved notification routes separately when egress must stop.

Procedure

  1. Create a protected change record naming the scope, reason, reviewer and maintenance expiry.
  2. Apply drain or pause with explicit reference and inspect current counts. Do not infer a reconciled ledger from empty Queued work.
  3. Complete maintenance under the appropriate storage/identity/release SOP.
  4. Generate a fresh five-minute resume plan only after no durable Dispatching work remains and verify passes. Inspect its full effect, digest and unknown exposure.
  5. Apply the plan with external acceptance reference. Add --acknowledge-unknown only after actual review; the option does not settle anything.

Verification / exit checks: Operating revision advances, eligible control remains accessible, current authority remains unchanged, and a permitted fixture request reaches the expected outcome.

Containment: Prefer Draining for planned shutdown. Use Paused for unsafe queued execution. Both preserve existing liabilities.

Rollback: Apply restrictive pause immediately if recovery fails. A state change, cancellation or settlement can make the previous resume plan stale; regenerate, never edit it.

Escalation: Incident commander must approve expansion of authority; operator reference is an attestation, not authenticated dual approval.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

Closure: Incident owner confirms outcome against fresh source evidence and updates customer-facing status. A cleared alarm, successful probe or HTTP acknowledgement alone does not close the incident.

Review cadence: Before production enablement, after source/contract change, and at least quarterly. Run on fixtures first.

R14 command shapes
python server.py --db "$DB" ops-status
python server.py --db "$DB" ops-drain --reason "Planned controlled maintenance" --reference "$CHANGE_REF"
# Use pause instead when queued dispatch is unsafe.
python server.py --db "$DB" ops-pause --reason "Contain uncertain execution" --reference "$CHANGE_REF"
python server.py --db "$DB" verify
python server.py --db "$DB" ops-resume-plan --out "$EVIDENCE/resume-plan.json"
# After independent inspection, while the plan is current:
python server.py --db "$DB" ops-resume --plan "$EVIDENCE/resume-plan.json" --reference "$ACCEPTANCE_REF"

SOP-06 · SOPCancel queued inference without fabricating upstream cancellationR14 current
Owner
Scoped service operator
Applies to
R14 current
Trigger
User withdraws a request or queued work should not dispatch.
Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Inspect current request state and source version. Ensure the request belongs to the authorized workspace.
  2. Distinguish Queued from Dispatching. Browser disconnect and response loss do not prove that no provider work occurred.

Procedure

  1. Open Request Inspector, review state and use Cancel queued request with explicit reason through authenticated API/console.
  2. Keep the same idempotency key if acknowledgement is lost. Retrieve existing receipt after reauthorization.

  4. Inspect request Not dispatched state, closed job, one release journal effect and absence of an attempt.

Verification / exit checks: Exactly one release, no new attempt, liability preserved for any already dispatched request and current authorization enforced on replay.

Containment: Pause admission if broader scope is unsafe, but do not assume that pause cancels outstanding work.

Rollback: Cancellation is not undone by editing the record. New desired work requires a new explicit authorized intent.

Escalation: Execution owner for inconsistent state; ledger owner for unexpected release amount.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

SOP-09 · SOPInvestigate and reconcile an Unknown financial outcomeR14 current; real invoices not integrated
Owner

Applies to

Diagnose

Procedure

Containment: Keep the reservation held until an authorized settlement. An acknowledged case, expired rate window or closed browser does not release it.

Rollback: No direct reversal/deletion. Incorrect recorded liability requires a deliberately designed corrective accounting path and governance review; R14 does not provide arbitrary adjustment commands.

Escalation: Ledger owner for disagreement; provider/commercial finance for unsupported evidence. Escalate one-hour unresolved exposure according to approved risk threshold.

Diagnose

SOP-12 · SOPQualify pricing and budget-bound changesCandidate and future real integration
Owner
FinOps and model owner
Applies to
Candidate and future real integration
Trigger
Price revision stale/missing or provider introduces new billable dimensions.
Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Review model/version/endpoint/region, tokenizer estimate, max output and all fees. R14 fixture-u2-u8-v1 charges synthetic units, not real tokens.
  2. Check pricing provenance, effective time, currency basis and whether retries, tools or cache creation incur additional liability.

Procedure

  1. Prepare new immutable price evidence and bounded estimation function with independently computed examples.
  2. Run representative input/output/edge-case tests, including unknown usage, cancellation and maximum output.
  3. Bind price revision to service contract and request evidence; perform a reviewed activation.
  4. Reconcile measured provider usage and aggregate billing before reducing safety margins.
  5. Display reservation estimate separately from observed accrual and invoice cost.

Verification / exit checks: Unknown pricing fails safely, admitted upper bounds cover qualified dimensions and historical records keep their original price revision.

Containment: Refuse new unpriced work under strict-budget contracts. Do not reinterpret absent usage as free or rely on a mutable online price map without revision control.

Rollback: Revert only to a still-valid approved price revision; otherwise pause relevant admission.

Escalation: FinOps for financial exposure, provider owner for ambiguous billing dimensions.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

Closure: Incident owner confirms outcome against fresh source evidence and updates customer-facing status. A cleared alarm, successful probe or HTTP acknowledgement alone does not close the incident.

Review cadence: Before production enablement, after source/contract change, and at least quarterly. Run on fixtures first.

SOP-14 · SOPRevoke compromised identity or workload credentialsR14 current
Owner
Identity/security owner
Applies to
R14 current
Trigger
Known credential loss, employee offboarding or revoked authority still accepted.
Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Distinguish local user/session, OIDC binding, workload grant and individual credential. Disabling a creator does not revoke independently approved workloads.
  2. List scoped active credentials without extracting raw token/hash values.

Procedure

  1. For OIDC binding, use identity-revoke with exact binding ID and reason. For workload or credential use the authenticated scoped revocation action.
  2. Recheck queued work authorization and stale approvals referencing the revoked binding.
  3. Rotate workload credentials through current reviewed grant, deliberately deliver the new token once through a secure channel, and verify old-token rejection.
  4. Sign out affected sessions; communicate that this does not necessarily end IdP session or cancel liabilities.
  5. Use a negative test before restoring access and record measured revocation propagation.

Verification / exit checks: Old authority is rejected before receipt replay and before dispatch; new intended authority works only in its approved service scope.

Containment: Revoke the compromised surface promptly. Pause affected execution if current authority cannot be trusted.

Rollback: Do not un-revoke a compromised token. Create fresh separately approved authority.

Escalation: Security for suspected external use, FinOps for requests dispatched before containment.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

Closure: Incident owner confirms outcome against fresh source evidence and updates customer-facing status. A cleared alarm, successful probe or HTTP acknowledgement alone does not close the incident.

Review cadence: Before production enablement, after source/contract change, and at least quarterly. Run on fixtures first.

R14 command shapes
python server.py --db "$DB" identity-revoke "$BINDING_ID" --reason "Compromised binding containment reference"

SOP-20 · SOPCreate and verify a protected backupR14 current
Owner
Storage owner
Applies to
R14 current
Trigger

Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. umask 077
python server.py --db "$DB" backup "$EVIDENCE/backup.sqlite3"
python server.py --db "$EVIDENCE/backup.sqlite3" verify

SOP-21 · SOPRestore into quarantine and reestablish authorityR14 current
Owner
Incident commander, storage and finance
Applies to
R14 current
Trigger

Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Choose a verified source backup and new absent destination. Establish recovery point and post-backup obligation list.
  2. Source must not be actively served. Identify all sessions, workloads, approvals, receiver routes and jobs whose historical authority must not revive.

Procedure

  1. Perform same-host drill first where possible and inspect its explicit checks.

  3. Verify financial graph, inspect Unknown outcomes, reconcile liabilities incurred after backup using external evidence.

Containment: Restore remains Recovery, services paused, tenants blocked, credentials/bindings revoked and notifications disabled. No worker in a drill.

Rollback: Keep the new branch quarantined and retain original source. Never fail back automatically to stale authority.

Escalation: Finance and security must approve unresolved liability and reauthorization before restoration becomes production traffic.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

Closure: Incident owner confirms outcome against fresh source evidence and updates customer-facing status. A cleared alarm, successful probe or HTTP acknowledgement alone does not close the incident.

python server.py restore "$BACKUP" "$NEW_DB"

python server.py --db "$NEW_DB" ops-status

SOP-27 · SOPRotate TLS certificates and notification signing keysR14 TLS edge and webhook receiver
Owner
Security and integration owner
Applies to
R14 TLS edge and webhook receiver
Trigger
Expiry warning, planned rotation or suspected key compromise.
Preconditions
Confirm environment, cell, source revision and incident/change reference. Use the least-privileged current identity. Do not paste secrets, prompts or private notes into a ticket. Use a protected evidence directory and UTC timestamps. Read-only SQL is for a trusted operator on a consistent snapshot, not a tenant-access API.

Diagnose

  1. Identify independent TLS termination, hostname trust, OIDC signing keys and webhook HMAC secret. They are not interchangeable.
  2. For notification key, route binds secret digest. Replacing environment value alone may block rather than seamlessly rotate.

Procedure

  1. Prepare new secrets via approved secret manager/environment injection; never put key bytes in shell arguments or docs.
  2. For receiver route changes, generate and inspect new exact-state route plan with domain/workspace/host/path/secret reference. Apply with external review and egress acknowledgement.
  3. Coordinate receiver trust overlap and retirement policy for pending original events; do not rewrite their payload or destination.
  4. Run explicit non-sensitive signed probe and actual certificate hostname/chain rejection tests.
  5. Retire old material only after documented pending-event handling and verified old-key rejection.

Verification / exit checks: Expected chain/hostname works, invalid certificate/signature fails, active route configuration matches review, no secret appears in telemetry.

Containment: On compromise disable route/revoke relevant identity. Avoid disabling TLS verification or accepting unsigned notifications.

Rollback: Disable new route and restore only a still-approved uncompromised configuration through review; old in-flight bytes cannot be recalled.

Escalation: Security for compromise; receiver owner for incompatible rotation or unresolved pending events.

Evidence: Record incident/change reference, scope, exact artifact and configuration revisions, observation time, diagnostic result hashes, actors, commands/actions taken, before/after state and unresolved liabilities. Never attach raw credentials or unrestricted database files.

Closure: Incident owner confirms outcome against fresh source evidence and updates customer-facing status. A cleared alarm, successful probe or HTTP acknowledgement alone does not close the incident.

Review cadence: Before production enablement, after source/contract change, and at least quarterly. Run on fixtures first.

DailyStorage/operationsVerify approved backup completion and available storage; review unexpected restarts.Actual backup verification receipt, not a green job start.
WeeklyService/securityReview unused or expiring workloads, stale approvals, access changes and emergency actions.Scoped grant report and corrective proposals; SQL-13 SQL-17 SQL-38
WeeklyProduct/SREReview failed critical journeys, uncertain acknowledgements, alert volume and manual effort.Cohort measures, not a synthetic benchmark score.
Monthly / after material changeStorage/SREQuarantined restore drill; review capacity, monitoring cardinality, silence expiry and detector thresholds.Hash-bound drill receipt and measured limits.
Each releaseRelease ownerPin artifacts/configuration, verify affected gates, canary actual behavior, review rollback.Exact-version gate evidence; no inherited CI success.
Quarterly or policy-definedGovernance/privacyReview data retention, receiver custody, owner coverage, legal holds and unresolved risks.Approved policy revisions and deletion/custody evidence.

A maintenance record identifies scope, expected impact, blocked actions, authorized containment, monitoring exclusions, backup/restore boundary and expiry. A fresh five-minute resume plan is invalidated by material source changes; do not keep retrying an old plan. Restrictions and obligations survive operator handoff. Use SOP-36 for the routine review and SOP-32 for rollout qualification.

23. Operational findings and prioritized remediation

Sixteen source-grounded findings identify the work between a useful reference and an accepted enterprise operating service. These are not newly claimed vulnerabilities or fixes deployed by C2. Priority reflects qualification risk, not a production incident severity.

OF-01 · FindingNo unified metrics emission in R14P1 qualification

P1 qualification
Risk
